    68	        ({ received: 0, triaged: 1, in_progress: 2, shipped: 3, wont_fix: 3 }[s] ?? -1)
    69	      const row = rows.find((r) => r.id === params.p_id)
    70	      if (
    71	        !row ||
    72	        row.last_emailed_stage === params.p_stage ||
    73	        rank(params.p_stage) <= rank(row.journey_stage ?? 'received')
    74	      ) {
    75	        return { data: [], error: null }
    76	      }
    77	      row.journey_stage = params.p_stage
    78	      row.last_emailed_stage = params.p_stage
    79	      updates.push({ rpc: fn, params })
    80	      return { data: [{ id: row.id }], error: null }
    81	    },
    82	    from(table) {
    83	      assert.equal(table, 'feedback')
    84	
    85	      return {
    86	        insert(values) {
    87	          return {
    88	            select() {
    89	              return {
    90	                async single() {
    91	                  const row = { id: `feedback-${nextId++}`, ...values }
    92	                  rows.push(row)
    93	                  inserts.push(values)
    94	                  return { data: { id: row.id }, error: null }
    95	                },
    96	              }
    97	            },
    98	          }
    99	        },
   100	        update(values) {
   101	          const filters = []
   102	          let orExpression = null
   103	
   104	          function applyUpdate() {
   105	            const matched = rows.filter((row) => matchesFilters(row, filters) && matchesOr(row, orExpression))
   106	            for (const row of matched) {
   107	              Object.assign(row, values)
   108	            }
   109	            updates.push({ values, filters: [...filters], orExpression, count: matched.length })
   110	            return matched
   111	          }
   112	
   113	          const builder = {
   114	            eq(column, value) {
   115	              filters.push({ column, value })
   116	              return builder
   117	            },
   118	            or(expression) {
   119	              orExpression = expression
   120	              return builder
   121	            },
   122	            async select() {
   123	              const matched = applyUpdate()
   124	              return { data: matched.map((row) => ({ id: row.id })), error: null }
   125	            },
   126	            then(resolve, reject) {
   127	              try {
   128	                resolve({ error: null, data: applyUpdate() })
   129	              } catch (error) {
   130	                reject(error)
   131	              }
   132	            },
   133	          }
   134	
   135	          return builder
   136	        },
   137	        select() {
   138	          const filters = []
   139	          const builder = {
   140	            eq(column, value) {
   141	              filters.push({ column, value })
   142	              return builder
   143	            },
   144	            async maybeSingle() {
   145	              const row = rows.find((candidate) => matchesFilters(candidate, filters)) || null
   146	              return { data: clone(row), error: null }
   147	            },
   148	          }
   149	
   150	          return builder
   151	        },
   152	      }
   153	    },
   154	  }
   155	
   156	  return { client, rows, inserts, updates }
   157	}
   158	
   159	function feedbackRequest(fields, headers = {}) {
   160	  const formData = new FormData()
   161	  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(fields)) {
   162	    formData.append(key, value)
   163	  }
   164	
   165	  return new Request('https://example.test/api/feedback', {
   166	    method: 'POST',
   167	    headers: {
   168	      'x-page-url': 'https://app.test/export?email=page@example.com',
   169	      'x-real-ip': `127.0.0.${Math.floor(Math.random() * 200) + 1}`,
   170	      'user-agent': 'node-test',
   171	      ...headers,
   172	    },
   173	    body: formData,
   174	  })
   175	}

   177	function feedbackDeps(options = {}) {
   178	  const supabase = options.supabase || createFakeSupabase()
   179	  const createIssueCalls = []
   180	  const confirmations = []
   181	
   182	  return {
   183	    supabase,
   184	    createIssueCalls,
   185	    confirmations,
   186	    deps: {
   187	      supabase: supabase.client,
   188	      env: {
   189	        SUPABASE_URL: 'https://supabase.test',
   190	        SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY: 'service-role',
   191	        GEMINI_API_KEY: 'gemini',
   192	        GITHUB_TOKEN: 'github-token',
   193	        GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO: 'owner/repo',
   194	        RESEND_API_KEY: 'resend-token',
   195	        ...options.env,
   196	      },
   197	      analyze: options.analyze || (async () => aiData),
   198	      createIssue: options.createIssue || (async (params) => {
   199	        const payload = buildFeedbackIssuePayload(params)
   200	        createIssueCalls.push({ params, payload })
   201	        return { number: 1, url: 'https://x/1' }
   202	      }),
   203	      sendConfirmation: async (params) => {
   204	        confirmations.push(params)
   205	      },
   206	    },
   207	  }
   208	}

   210	function webhookRequest(payload, secret, headers = {}) {
   211	  const raw = JSON.stringify(payload)
   212	  const signature = 'sha256=' + createHmac('sha256', secret).update(raw).digest('hex')
   213	
   214	  return new Request('https://example.test/api/feedback/webhook', {
   215	    method: 'POST',
   216	    headers: {
   217	      'x-github-event': 'issues',
   218	      'x-hub-signature-256': signature,
   219	      ...headers,
   220	    },
   221	    body: raw,
   222	  })
   223	}

   239	      sendStage: async (params) => {

   473	test('GitHub issue payload redacts adversarial PII and sanitizes screenshot URLs', () => {
   474	  const secrets = [
   475	    'user＠example.com',
   476	    'alice [at] example [dot] com',
   477	    'bob(at)mail(dot)co',
   478	    '555[.]123[.]4567',
   479	    '+1 (555) 123 4567',
   480	    'https://u:p@host.com/x?token=abc#frag',
   481	    'access_token=SEKRET123',
   482	  ]
   483	
   484	  for (const secret of secrets) {
   485	    const payload = buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   486	      token: 'token',
   487	      repo: 'owner/repo',
   488	      aiData: {
   489	        ...aiData,
   490	        title: `Problem ${secret}`,
   491	        short_summary: `Summary ${secret}`,
   492	        key_details: [`Detail ${secret}`],
   493	      },
   494	      messageRaw: `Message ${secret}`,
   495	      pageUrl: `https://u:p@app.test/path?token=${encodeURIComponent(secret)}#frag`,
   496	      screenshotUrl: 'https://storage.test/feedback.png?X-Amz-Signature=SEKRET123#frag',
   497	    })
   498	
   499	    const text = `${payload.title}\n${payload.body}`
   500	    assert.equal(text.includes(secret), false, secret)
   501	    assert.equal(text.includes(secret.normalize('NFKC')), false, secret)
   502	    assert.equal(text.includes('SEKRET123'), false)
   503	    assert.equal(text.includes('X-Amz-Signature'), false)
   504	    assert.equal(text.includes('u:p@'), false)
   505	  }
   506	
   507	  const unsafeScreenshotPayload = buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   508	    token: 'token',
   509	    repo: 'owner/repo',
   510	    aiData,
   511	    messageRaw: 'Screenshot path token',
   512	    pageUrl: 'https://app.test/path',
   513	    screenshotUrl: 'https://storage.test/feedback/0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef.png?X-Amz-Signature=SEKRET123',
   514	  })
   515	
   516	  assert.equal(unsafeScreenshotPayload.body.includes('[screenshot omitted: unsafe URL]'), true)
   517	  assert.equal(unsafeScreenshotPayload.body.includes('0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef'), false)
   518	})

   520	test('GitHub issue body defangs markdown / mention / control-char injection', () => {
   521	  const mk = (s) => buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   522	    token: 't', repo: 'o/r',
   523	    aiData: { ...aiData, title: s, short_summary: s, key_details: [s] },
   524	    messageRaw: s, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x',
   525	  })
   526	  // @mention must not stay a live GitHub mention
   527	  let b = `${mk('ping @federicodeponte now').title}\n${mk('ping @federicodeponte now').body}`
   528	  assert.equal(/@federicodeponte/.test(b.replace(/\u200B/g, '')) && !b.includes('@\u200Bfedericodeponte'), false)
   529	  assert.equal(b.includes('@\u200Bfedericodeponte'), true)
   530	  // issue cross-ref / auto-close keyword defanged
   531	  b = mk('Closes #1 and fixes #2').body
   532	  assert.equal(b.includes('#\u200B1') && b.includes('#\u200B2'), true)
   533	  // fenced code breakout defanged
   534	  assert.equal(mk('```js\\nx\\n```').body.includes('`\u200B`\u200B`\u200B'), true)
   535	  // raw HTML escaped (no live tag)
   536	  b = mk('<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>').body
   537	  assert.equal(/<img|<script/i.test(b), false)
   538	  assert.equal(b.includes('&lt;img'), true)
   539	  // inline link / image (phishing + tracking pixel) defanged
   540	  b = mk('![p](https://evil.test/t.png) and [go](https://evil.test)').body
   541	  assert.equal(/!\[[^\]]*\]\(http/.test(b), false)
   542	  assert.equal(/\[[^\]]*\]\(https?:/.test(b), false)
   543	  // control / bidi (Trojan-Source) stripped
   544	  const evil = `a${String.fromCharCode(0)}b${String.fromCharCode(0x202e)}c${String.fromCharCode(0xfeff)}d`
   545	  b = mk(evil).body
   546	  const CTRL = /[\u0000-\u0008\u000B\u000C\u000E-\u001F\u007F-\u009F\u200E\u200F\u202A-\u202E\u2066-\u2069\uFEFF]/
   547	  assert.equal(CTRL.test(b), false)
   548	  assert.equal(b.includes('abcd'), true)
   549	})

   551	test('Codex 2026-05-19 findings: ReDoS-bounded, Unicode-email, phone-extension, comprehensive control-strip, URL no-autolink', () => {
   552	  const mk = (s) => buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   553	    token: 't', repo: 'o/r',
   554	    aiData: { ...aiData, title: s, short_summary: s, key_details: [s] },
   555	    messageRaw: s, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x',
   556	  })
   557	  // #1 ReDoS: 10KB pathological input must complete fast (<500ms)
   558	  const huge = '@'.repeat(10000) + 'a@b' + '.'.repeat(10000)
   559	  const t0 = Date.now()
   560	  mk(huge)
   561	  const ms = Date.now() - t0
   562	  assert.ok(ms < 500, `ReDoS guard: redaction took ${ms}ms on 10KB pathological input`)
   563	
   564	  // #3 Unicode-dot email (U+3002 ideographic full stop)
   565	  let b = mk('contact user@example。com today').body
   566	  assert.equal(b.includes('user@example'), false, 'U+3002 email leaked')
   567	  assert.equal(b.includes('[redacted email]'), true)
   568	  // #3 CJK domain email
   569	  b = mk('mail 例え@例え.テスト thanks').body
   570	  assert.equal(b.includes('[redacted email]'), true, 'CJK-domain email not redacted')
   571	  // #3 phone+extension glued
   572	  b = mk('call 5558675309x123 today').body
   573	  assert.equal(b.includes('5558675309'), false, 'phone-with-extension leaked')
   574	  assert.equal(b.includes('[redacted phone]'), true)
   575	
   576	  // #4 invisible-format chars beyond the old allowlist
   577	  const invis = `AB${String.fromCharCode(0x2028)}C${String.fromCharCode(0x2029)}D${String.fromCharCode(0x180E)}E${String.fromCharCode(0x2060)}F${String.fromCharCode(0xFEFF)}G`
   578	  b = mk(invis).body
   579	  for (const cp of [0x2028, 0x2029, 0x180E, 0x2060, 0xFEFF]) {
   580	    const ch = String.fromCharCode(cp)
   581	    assert.equal(b.includes(ch), false, `control char U+${cp.toString(16)} survived strip`)
   582	  }
   583	  assert.equal(b.includes('ABCDEFG'), true, 'legitimate letters preserved')
   584	
   585	  // #2 URLs must NOT autolink: sanitizeUrl inserts a zwsp after protocol
   586	  b = mk('see https://evil.test/path for details').body
   587	  // bare "https://evil.test" should no longer appear without a zwsp inside
   588	  assert.equal(/https:\/\/evil\.test/.test(b), false, 'bare URL still autolinks')
   589	  assert.ok(b.includes('https:'+String.fromCharCode(0x200B)+'//'), 'expected defanged URL with zwsp after protocol')
   590	})
   591	
   592	test('Codex round-6 findings: legit multi-segment URL renders; non-http scheme omits; paren-in-URL cannot break out', () => {
   593	  const ai = { ...aiData }
   594	  const mk = (ss) => buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   595	    token: 't', repo: 'o/r', aiData: ai, messageRaw: 'm', pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x', screenshotUrl: ss,
   596	  }).body
   597	  // #1 regression guard: a realistic Supabase public URL must STILL render
   598	  // (the round-5 entropy regex was too greedy across slashes)
   599	  const b1 = mk('https://project.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/feedback/1700000000000-abcde.png')
   600	  assert.ok(/!\[Feedback screenshot\]\(http/.test(b1), 'legitimate Supabase URL should render')
   601	  // #2 non-http schemes never embed (data: payload leak; javascript:)
   602	  for (const u of ['data:image/png;base64,U0VDUkVUX1RPS0VO', 'javascript:alert(1)', 'mailto:x@y']) {
   603	    const b = mk(u)
   604	    assert.equal(b.includes(u.split(':')[1]?.slice(0, 10) || u), false, `non-http URL embedded: ${u}`)
   605	    assert.equal(b.includes('[screenshot omitted: unsafe URL]'), true)
   606	  }
   607	  // #3 markdown image breakout via `)` in URL must not yield extra images
   608	  const b3 = mk('https://cdn.test/a)![x](https://evil.test/pixel.png')
   609	  assert.equal(/!\[x\]\(https:\/\/evil/.test(b3), false, 'paren-injection rendered extra image')
   610	})
   611	
   612	test('Codex round-5 findings: localized-digit phones redact; base32/percent-encoded screenshot URLs omitted', () => {
   613	  const mk = (s, ss) => buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   614	    token: 't', repo: 'o/r',
   615	    aiData: { ...aiData, title: s, short_summary: s, key_details: [s] },
   616	    messageRaw: s, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x', screenshotUrl: ss,
   617	  }).body
   618	  // Localized digit scripts (Arabic-Indic, Devanagari, Bengali) must redact
   619	  // as phone like ASCII.
   620	  for (const ph of ['٥٥٥١٢٣٤٥٦٧', '५५५१२३४५६७']) {
   621	    const b = mk(`call ${ph} now`)
   622	    assert.equal(b.includes(ph), false, `localized digits leaked: ${ph}`)
   623	    assert.equal(b.includes('[redacted phone]'), true)
   624	  }
   625	  // Screenshot path with base32 padding or percent-encoding must be omitted.
   626	  for (const ss of [
   627	    'https://cdn.test/files/MFRGGZDFMZTWQ2LKNNWG23TPOI======.png',
   628	    'https://cdn.test/files/abcdEFGHijklMNOPqrstUVWX%2Fyz012345%2B6789%3D.png',
   629	  ]) {
   630	    const b = mk('shot', ss)
   631	    assert.equal(/!\[Feedback screenshot\]\(http/.test(b), false, `unsafe screenshot URL rendered: ${ss}`)
   632	    assert.equal(b.includes('[screenshot omitted: unsafe URL]'), true)
   633	  }
   634	})
   635	
   636	test('Codex round-4 findings: attacker default-ignorable inside email/URL must be stripped BEFORE redaction; bare GTIN-length phones must redact', () => {
   637	  const mk = (s) => buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   638	    token: 't', repo: 'o/r',
   639	    aiData: { ...aiData, title: s, short_summary: s, key_details: [s] },
   640	    messageRaw: s, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x',
   641	  }).body
   642	  const ZWSP = String.fromCharCode(0x200B)
   643	  // attacker inserts U+200B inside the email -> stripControl runs BEFORE
   644	  // EMAIL_RE so redaction still fires (Codex round 4 #1)
   645	  for (const evil of [
   646	    `contact leak@example.${ZWSP}com now`,
   647	    `contact leak@exa${ZWSP}mple.com now`,
   648	    `mail user${ZWSP}@example.com x`,
   649	  ]) {
   650	    const b = mk(evil)
   651	    assert.equal(b.includes('leak@'), false, `mid-zwsp email leaked: ${JSON.stringify(evil)}`)
   652	    assert.equal(b.includes('example.com'), false, `mid-zwsp email domain leaked`)
   653	  }
   654	  // bare 8/12/13/14 digit numbers must redact (GTIN_RE removed; phone-vs-GTIN
   655	  // indistinguishable, redaction wins — Codex round 4 #2)
   656	  for (const ph of ['55512345', '447911123456', '4915112345678', '00012345600012']) {
   657	    const b = mk(`call ${ph}`)
   658	    assert.equal(b.includes(ph), false, `bare ${ph.length}-digit number leaked as phone`)
   659	    assert.equal(b.includes('[redacted phone]'), true)
   660	  }
   661	})
   662	
   663	test('Codex round-3 findings: combining-mark emails, punycode TLD, www. defang, date/IPv4/ISBN/GTIN survive, default-ignorable stripped, cap-after-NFKC', () => {
   664	  const mk = (s) => buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   665	    token: 't', repo: 'o/r',
   666	    aiData: { ...aiData, title: s, short_summary: s, key_details: [s] },
   667	    messageRaw: s, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x',
   668	  })
   669	  for (const eml of ['عَرَبِيّ@example.com', 'user@مِثال.إختبار', 'user@example.xn--p1ai']) {
   670	    const b = mk(eml).body
   671	    assert.equal(b.includes(eml), false, `email ${eml} leaked`)
   672	    assert.equal(b.includes('[redacted email]'), true, `${eml} not redacted`)
   673	  }
   674	  let b = mk('see www.evil.test/path').body
   675	  assert.equal(/\bwww\.evil\.test/.test(b), false, 'bare www. still autolinks')
   676	  // Hyphenated ISO date / IPv4 / hyphenated ISBN-13 survive (have shape).
   677	  // Bare GTIN (no separators) is intentionally NOT kept: indistinguishable
   678	  // from international phones; redaction wins (Codex round 4 #2).
   679	  for (const id of ['2026-05-19', '192.168.100.200', '978-0-306-40615-7']) {
   680	    b = mk(`reference ${id} here`).body
   681	    assert.equal(b.includes(id), true, `identifier ${id} was over-redacted`)
   682	  }
   683	  const evil = `A${String.fromCharCode(0x034F)}B${String.fromCharCode(0xFE0F)}C${String.fromCharCode(0xFE00)}D`
   684	  b = mk(evil).body
   685	  for (const cp of [0x034F, 0xFE00, 0xFE0F]) {
   686	    assert.equal(b.includes(String.fromCharCode(cp)), false, `U+${cp.toString(16)} survived`)
   687	  }
   688	  assert.equal(b.includes('ABCD'), true, 'legitimate letters preserved')
   689	  const big = 'ﷺ'.repeat(8192)
   690	  const t0 = Date.now()
   691	  mk(big)
   692	  const ms = Date.now() - t0
   693	  assert.ok(ms < 500, `cap-after-NFKC: ${ms}ms (expected < 500)`)
   694	})
   695	
   696	test('phone redaction preserves alphanumeric ids but still redacts real phones', () => {
   697	  // Defensible contract: identifiers where digits are glued to letters (the
   698	  // real reported case, e.g. CMB1779152387) must survive so the issue stays
   699	  // actionable. Residual (documented, by design): a PURE numeric group split
   700	  // by separators (e.g. ORD-2024-998877 -> the 2024-998877 part) is
   701	  // indistinguishable from a phone by pattern alone, so it stays redacted as
   702	  // the privacy-safe default. Glued alphanumerics are the precision win.
   703	  const keepIds = ['CMB1779152387', 'ABC123456789', 'sha256:9f8e7d6c5b4a3210', 'v12345678', 'INV-2024-X7Q9']
   704	  const realPhones = ['555-867-5309', '+1 (555) 123 4567', '5558675309', '555.867.5309', '+44 20 7946 0958']
   705	
   706	  for (const id of keepIds) {
   707	    const p = buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   708	      token: 't', repo: 'o/r', aiData: { ...aiData, title: `Broken ${id}`, short_summary: `re ${id}`, key_details: [`id ${id}`] },
   709	      messageRaw: `The export for ${id} fails`, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x',
   710	    })
   711	    const text = `${p.title}\n${p.body}`
   712	    assert.equal(text.includes(id), true, `id should survive: ${id}`)
   713	  }
   714	
   715	  for (const ph of realPhones) {
   716	    const p = buildFeedbackIssuePayload({
   717	      token: 't', repo: 'o/r', aiData: { ...aiData, title: 'Call me', short_summary: `reach ${ph}`, key_details: [`phone ${ph}`] },
   718	      messageRaw: `Please call ${ph} about this`, pageUrl: 'https://app.test/x',
   719	    })
   720	    const text = `${p.title}\n${p.body}`
   721	    assert.equal(text.includes(ph), false, `phone should be redacted: ${ph}`)
   722	    assert.equal(text.includes('[redacted phone]'), true, `expected redaction marker for: ${ph}`)
   723	  }
   724	})
   725	
   726	test('webhook ignores signed issue events from a different configured repo', async () => {
   727	  const setup = webhookDeps([
   728	    {
   729	      id: 'feedback-1',
   730	      submitter_email: 'person@example.com',
   731	      subscribe: true,
   732	      github_issue_number: 1,
   733	      github_issue_url: 'https://x/1',
   734	      github_repo: 'owner/repo',
   735	      journey_stage: 'received',
   736	      last_emailed_stage: null,
   737	      ai_data: { title: 'Export issue' },
   738	    },
   739	  ], {
   740	    env: {
   741	      GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO: 'owner/repo',
   742	    },
   743	  })
   744	
   745	  const response = await handleWebhook(
   746	    webhookRequest({
   747	      ...triagedPayload,
   748	      repository: { full_name: 'attacker/repo' },
   749	    }, 'secret'),
   750	    setup.deps,
   751	  )
   752	  const body = await response.json()
   753	
   754	  assert.equal(response.status, 200)
   755	  assert.deepEqual(body, { ignored: true })
   756	  assert.equal(setup.stages.length, 0)
   757	  assert.equal(setup.supabase.rows[0].journey_stage, 'received')
   758	})
   759	
   760	test('webhook concurrent duplicate deliveries send one stage email', async () => {
   761	  const setup = webhookDeps([
   762	    {
   763	      id: 'feedback-1',
   764	      submitter_email: 'person@example.com',
   765	      subscribe: true,
   766	      github_issue_number: 1,
   767	      github_issue_url: 'https://x/1',
   768	      github_repo: 'owner/repo',
   769	      journey_stage: 'received',
   770	      last_emailed_stage: null,
   771	      ai_data: { title: 'Export issue' },
   772	    },
   773	  ])
   774	
   775	  await Promise.all([
   776	    handleWebhook(webhookRequest(triagedPayload, 'secret'), setup.deps),
   777	    handleWebhook(webhookRequest(triagedPayload, 'secret'), setup.deps),
   778	  ])
   779	
   780	  assert.equal(setup.stages.length, 1)
   781	  assert.equal(setup.supabase.rows[0].journey_stage, 'triaged')
   782	  assert.equal(setup.supabase.rows[0].last_emailed_stage, 'triaged')
   783	})

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# OpenFeedbackLayer
     2	
     3	Open-source feedback widget with AI classification. A drop-in React component that captures user feedback, automatically classifies it using Gemini AI, and stores it in Supabase.
     4	
     5	## Features
     6	
     7	- **Floating feedback button** - Non-intrusive, appears in corner of screen
     8	- **Screenshot support** - Paste, drag & drop, or click to attach images
     9	- **AI classification** - Automatically categorizes feedback (bug/feature/question/billing/praise)
    10	- **Feature area detection** - AI identifies which part of your product is mentioned
    11	- **Priority scoring** - P0/P1/P2 based on urgency
    12	- **Bot protection** - Honeypot field + rate limiting
    13	- **Email notifications** - Get notified via Resend on new feedback
    14	- **Supabase storage** - Screenshots stored in Supabase Storage
    15	
    16	## Quick Start
    17	
    18	### 1. Install the package
    19	
    20	```bash
    21	npm install openfeedbacklayer @supabase/supabase-js
    22	# or
    23	pnpm add openfeedbacklayer @supabase/supabase-js
    24	# or
    25	yarn add openfeedbacklayer @supabase/supabase-js
    26	```
    27	
    28	### 2. Add the widget to your app
    29	
    30	```tsx
    31	// app/layout.tsx or any layout component
    32	import { FeedbackWidget } from 'openfeedbacklayer'
    33	
    34	export default function Layout({ children }) {
    35	  return (
    36	    <html>
    37	      <body>
    38	        {children}
    39	        <FeedbackWidget />
    40	      </body>
    41	    </html>
    42	  )
    43	}
    44	```
    45	
    46	### 3. Set up the API route
    47	
    48	Copy the API route template to your Next.js app:
    49	
    50	```bash
    51	cp node_modules/openfeedbacklayer/src/api/feedback/route.ts app/api/feedback/route.ts
    52	```
    53	
    54	Or create `app/api/feedback/route.ts` manually - see [API Route Template](./src/api/feedback/route.ts).
    55	
    56	### 4. Set up Supabase
    57	
    58	Run the migrations in order in your Supabase SQL Editor:
    59	
    60	```bash
    61	ls node_modules/openfeedbacklayer/supabase/migrations
    62	```
    63	
    64	- `001_create_feedback.sql` and `002_feedback_journey.sql` are required. They
    65	  apply on any Postgres (no Supabase-specific objects).
    66	- `004_claim_stage_rpc.sql` is required if you use the subscriber journey
    67	  (GitHub webhook stage emails). Without it the webhook cannot claim a stage
    68	  and subscriber update emails are silently not sent.
    69	- `003_screenshot_storage.sql` is optional: run it only if you use screenshot
    70	  attachments. It requires Supabase Storage.
    71	
    72	Apply them in numeric order (`001`, `002`, `004`; `003` whenever you enable
    73	screenshots).
    74	
    75	Or see [Migration SQL](./supabase/migrations).
    76	
    77	### 5. Add environment variables
    78	
    79	```env
    80	# .env.local
    81	
    82	# Required
    83	SUPABASE_URL=https://xxx.supabase.co
    84	SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=eyJxxx
    85	
    86	# For AI classification
    87	GEMINI_API_KEY=AIzaSyxxx
    88	
    89	# Optional: Email notifications
    90	RESEND_API_KEY=re_xxx
    91	FEEDBACK_NOTIFY_EMAIL=you@example.com
    92	RESEND_FROM_EMAIL=feedback@yourdomain.com
    93	
    94	# Optional: GitHub feedback journey
    95	GITHUB_TOKEN=github_pat_xxx
    96	GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO=owner/name
    97	GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET=change-me
    98	```
    99	
   100	For GitHub issue creation and subscriber email updates, install `@octokit/rest` and `resend` in the host app when those features are enabled.
   101	
   102	## Configuration
   103	
   104	### Widget Props
   105	
   106	```tsx
   107	<FeedbackWidget
   108	  apiEndpoint="/api/feedback"       // API endpoint (default: /api/feedback)
   109	  projectId="my-app"                // For multi-project setups
   110	  position="bottom-right"           // bottom-right | bottom-left | top-right | top-left
   111	  primaryColor="#2563eb"            // Button color
   112	  buttonText="Feedback"             // Tooltip text
   113	  placeholder="Describe your issue..." // Input placeholder
   114	  collectEmail={true}               // Collect optional subscriber email
   115	  emailPlaceholder="Your email..."  // Email input placeholder
   116	  onSubmit={(data) => {}}           // Callback after submit
   117	  onError={(error) => {}}           // Error callback
   118	/>
   119	```
   120	
   121	## Feedback journey (GitHub + subscriber updates)
   122	
   123	Set `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO` to auto-open a GitHub issue for each feedback item. Install `@octokit/rest` and `resend` in the host app when GitHub issue creation and subscriber emails are enabled.
   124	
   125	To email subscribers as feedback moves through the journey, configure a GitHub webhook:
   126	
   127	- URL: `/api/feedback/webhook`
   128	- Content type: `application/json`
   129	- Events: Issues
   130	- Secret: `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`
   131	
   132	Subscribers receive concise updates for received, triaged, in progress, shipped, and won't fix stages. Submitter email is stored only in Supabase and is never added to the public GitHub issue. GitHub issue redaction is best-effort defense-in-depth, not a guarantee, because the issue body can still include user-typed content.
   133	
   134	### AI Classification Output
   135	
   136	The AI returns structured data:
   137	
   138	```json
   139	{
   140	  "title": "Export button not working",
   141	  "short_summary": "User reports the export button has no effect when clicked.",
   142	  "key_details": ["export button", "no response", "dashboard page"],
   143	  "suggested_category": "bug",
   144	  "suggested_feature_area": "export",
   145	  "suggested_priority": "high",
   146	  "steps": ["Go to dashboard", "Click export button"],
   147	  "expected": "CSV file should download",
   148	  "confidence": 0.95,
   149	  "clarifying_questions": []
   150	}
   151	```
   152	
   153	### Categories
   154	
   155	- `bug` - Something is broken
   156	- `feature` - Request for new functionality
   157	- `question` - How-to or support query
   158	- `billing` - Payment/subscription issues
   159	- `praise` - Positive feedback
   160	- `other` - Doesn't fit above
   161	
   162	### Priority
   163	
   164	- `high` → P0 - Blocking/urgent
   165	- `medium` → P1 - Important
   166	- `low` → P2 - Nice to have
   167	
   168	### Feature Area
   169	
   170	Free-form string detected by AI, e.g.:
   171	- "export", "upload", "dashboard", "billing", "login", "UI", "performance"
   172	
   173	## Database Schema
   174	
   175	```sql
   176	CREATE TABLE feedback (
   177	  id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
   178	  page_url TEXT NOT NULL,
   179	  user_agent TEXT,
   180	  project_id TEXT,
   181	  message_raw TEXT NOT NULL,
   182	  screenshot_url TEXT,
   183	  ai_data JSONB,
   184	  status TEXT DEFAULT 'new',
   185	  submitter_email TEXT,
   186	  subscribe BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,
   187	  github_issue_number INTEGER,
   188	  github_issue_url TEXT,
   189	  github_repo TEXT,
   190	  journey_stage TEXT DEFAULT 'received',
   191	  last_emailed_stage TEXT,
   192	  created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
   193	  updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
   194	);
   195	```
   196	
   197	## Querying Feedback
   198	
   199	```sql
   200	-- Get all bugs by priority
   201	SELECT * FROM feedback
   202	WHERE ai_data->>'suggested_category' = 'bug'
   203	ORDER BY
   204	  CASE ai_data->>'suggested_priority'
   205	    WHEN 'high' THEN 1
   206	    WHEN 'medium' THEN 2
   207	    ELSE 3
   208	  END;
   209	
   210	-- Get feedback by feature area
   211	SELECT * FROM feedback
   212	WHERE ai_data->>'suggested_feature_area' = 'export';
   213	
   214	-- Count by category
   215	SELECT
   216	  ai_data->>'suggested_category' as category,
   217	  COUNT(*) as count
   218	FROM feedback
   219	GROUP BY ai_data->>'suggested_category';
   220	```
   221	
   222	## Security
   223	
   224	- **Honeypot field** - Hidden input that bots fill, humans don't (best-effort)
   225	- **Rate limiting** - 10 requests/IP/minute. **Deploy behind a trusted
   226	  proxy/CDN** (Vercel, Cloudflare, nginx) that overwrites `x-forwarded-for`
   227	  with the real client IP. Exposed directly, the header is client-spoofable
   228	  and the limit can be bypassed.
   229	- **Supabase RLS** - Enable Row Level Security for access control
   230	- **Server-only RPC** - `claim_feedback_stage` is REVOKEd from
   231	  PUBLIC/anon/authenticated; only `service_role` may call it
   232	- **Untrusted-text defanging** - feedback text is redacted (PII) and defanged
   233	  (markdown / @mention / control-char) before it enters a GitHub issue
   234	- **No credentials exposed** - All API keys are server-side only
   235	
   236	## Cost
   237	
   238	Using Gemini 2.5 Flash Lite:
   239	- ~$0.10 per 1M input tokens
   240	- ~$0.40 per 1M output tokens
   241	- Typical feedback: ~200 tokens = **$0.00002 per feedback**
   242	
   243	## Tech Stack
   244	
   245	- React 18+
   246	- Next.js 13+ (App Router)
   247	- Supabase (PostgreSQL + Storage)
   248	- Google Gemini 2.5 Flash Lite
   249	- Resend (optional, for emails)
   250	
   251	## License
   252	
   253	MIT
   254	
   255	## Contributing
   256	
   257	PRs welcome! Please open an issue first to discuss changes.
   258	
   259	---
   260	
   261	Made with ❤️ by [SCAILE Technologies](https://scaile.tech)

    59	{
    60	  "name": "openfeedbacklayer-nextjs-example",
    61	  "private": true,
    62	  "version": "0.0.0",
    63	  "scripts": {
    64	    "dev": "next dev",
    65	    "build": "next build",
    66	    "start": "next start"
    67	  },
    68	  "dependencies": {
    69	    "next": "15.5.18",
    70	    "openfeedbacklayer": "file:../..",
    71	    "react": "18.2.0",
    72	    "react-dom": "18.2.0"
    73	  },
    74	  "devDependencies": {
    75	    "@types/node": "20.19.41",
    76	    "@types/react": "18.2.79",
    77	    "@types/react-dom": "18.2.25",
    78	    "typescript": "5.3.3"
    79	  }
    80	}
    81	/**

-Or see [Migration SQL](./supabase/migrations/001_create_feedback.sql).
+- `001_create_feedback.sql` and `002_feedback_journey.sql` are required. They
+  apply on any Postgres (no Supabase-specific objects).
+- `004_claim_stage_rpc.sql` is required if you use the subscriber journey
+  (GitHub webhook stage emails). Without it the webhook cannot claim a stage
+  and subscriber update emails are silently not sent.
+- `003_screenshot_storage.sql` is optional: run it only if you use screenshot
+  attachments. It requires Supabase Storage.
+
+Apply them in numeric order (`001`, `002`, `004`; `003` whenever you enable
+screenshots).
+
+Or see [Migration SQL](./supabase/migrations).
 
 ### 5. Add environment variables

 # Optional: Email notifications

 FEEDBACK_NOTIFY_EMAIL=you@example.com

 ```
 
+For GitHub issue creation and subscriber email updates, install `@octokit/rest` and `resend` in the host app when those features are enabled.
+
 ## Configuration
 
 ### Widget Props
@@ -93,11 +111,26 @@ FEEDBACK_NOTIFY_EMAIL=you@example.com
   primaryColor="#2563eb"            // Button color
   buttonText="Feedback"             // Tooltip text
   placeholder="Describe your issue..." // Input placeholder
+  collectEmail={true}               // Collect optional subscriber email
+  emailPlaceholder="Your email..."  // Email input placeholder
   onSubmit={(data) => {}}           // Callback after submit
   onError={(error) => {}}           // Error callback
 />
 ```
 
+## Feedback journey (GitHub + subscriber updates)
+
+Set `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO` to auto-open a GitHub issue for each feedback item. Install `@octokit/rest` and `resend` in the host app when GitHub issue creation and subscriber emails are enabled.
+
+To email subscribers as feedback moves through the journey, configure a GitHub webhook:
+
+- URL: `/api/feedback/webhook`
+- Content type: `application/json`
+- Events: Issues
+- Secret: `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`
+
+Subscribers receive concise updates for received, triaged, in progress, shipped, and won't fix stages. Submitter email is stored only in Supabase and is never added to the public GitHub issue. GitHub issue redaction is best-effort defense-in-depth, not a guarantee, because the issue body can still include user-typed content.
+
 ### AI Classification Output
 
 The AI returns structured data:
@@ -149,6 +182,13 @@ CREATE TABLE feedback (
   screenshot_url TEXT,
   ai_data JSONB,
   status TEXT DEFAULT 'new',
+  submitter_email TEXT,
+  subscribe BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,
+  github_issue_number INTEGER,
+  github_issue_url TEXT,
+  github_repo TEXT,
+  journey_stage TEXT DEFAULT 'received',
+  last_emailed_stage TEXT,
   created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
   updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
 );
@@ -181,9 +221,16 @@ GROUP BY ai_data->>'suggested_category';
 
 ## Security
 
-- **Honeypot field** - Hidden input that bots fill, humans don't
-- **Rate limiting** - 10 requests per IP per minute
+- **Honeypot field** - Hidden input that bots fill, humans don't (best-effort)
+- **Rate limiting** - 10 requests/IP/minute. **Deploy behind a trusted
+  proxy/CDN** (Vercel, Cloudflare, nginx) that overwrites `x-forwarded-for`
+  with the real client IP. Exposed directly, the header is client-spoofable
+  and the limit can be bypassed.
 - **Supabase RLS** - Enable Row Level Security for access control
+- **Server-only RPC** - `claim_feedback_stage` is REVOKEd from
+  PUBLIC/anon/authenticated; only `service_role` may call it
+- **Untrusted-text defanging** - feedback text is redacted (PII) and defanged
+  (markdown / @mention / control-char) before it enters a GitHub issue
 - **No credentials exposed** - All API keys are server-side only
 
 ## Cost

+  submitter_email TEXT,
+  subscribe BOOLEAN DEFAULT false NOT NULL,
+  github_issue_number INTEGER,
+  github_issue_url TEXT,

+-- in 003_screenshot_storage.sql so this core schema applies on any Postgres.
diff --git a/supabase/migrations/002_feedback_journey.sql b/supabase/migrations/002_feedback_journey.sql
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..d4bb7ad
--- /dev/null
+++ b/supabase/migrations/002_feedback_journey.sql
@@ -0,0 +1,20 @@
+-- OpenFeedbackLayer Feedback Journey
+-- Adds optional submitter updates and GitHub issue tracking.
+
+ALTER TABLE feedback
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS submitter_email TEXT,
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS subscribe BOOLEAN DEFAULT false NOT NULL,
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS github_issue_number INTEGER,
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS github_issue_url TEXT,
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS github_repo TEXT,
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS journey_stage TEXT DEFAULT 'received' NOT NULL
+    CHECK (journey_stage IN ('received','triaged','in_progress','shipped','wont_fix')),
+  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS last_emailed_stage TEXT;
+
+DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_feedback_issue_number;
+
+CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_feedback_repo_issue
+  ON feedback(github_repo, github_issue_number)
+  WHERE github_issue_number IS NOT NULL;
+
+COMMENT ON COLUMN feedback.journey_stage IS 'OpenFeedbackLayer feedback journey stage for GitHub issue and subscriber update flow';

    64	## P1 #7 — Fix 5 atomic claim uses .update().or() — BREAKS on real PostgREST (found 2026-05-19 via combined real-stack flow)
    65	Evidence: PATCH /feedback?id=eq.X&or=(last_emailed_stage.is.null,last_emailed_stage.neq.triaged)
    66	=> HTTP 400 PG 42703. eq/neq/is.null alone on PATCH = 200; or= on PATCH = 400;
    67	or= on GET = 200. supabase-js .update().eq().or().select() in webhook-core.ts:107-115
    68	therefore never advances journey_stage / never sends stage email against real
    69	Supabase. 26 fake-supabase tests + adversarial reviews + first "6/6 live"
    70	missed it (all used a JS fake that models .or() in memory). The subscribe
    71	journey (core feature) was non-functional end-to-end. Fix approach (now RESOLVED, see below): 
    72	atomic RPC claim_feedback_stage (IS DISTINCT FROM, handles NULL, one statement).

   121	### #8 resolution
   122	Phone candidate regex now requires a standalone token boundary
   123	(?<![A-Za-z0-9]) ... (?![A-Za-z0-9]) so digits glued to letters
   124	(CMB1779152387, ABC123456789, v12345678, sha refs) are preserved while real
   125	phones (555-867-5309, +1 (555) 123 4567, 5558675309, +44 ...) still redact.
   126	Documented residual: a pure-numeric group split only by separators
   127	(ORD-2024-998877 -> 2024-998877) is regex-indistinguishable from a phone and
   128	stays redacted as the privacy-safe default. Proven by the real built
   129	buildFeedbackIssuePayload (the exact fn production createFeedbackIssue uses,
   130	chain verified in today's combined flow). 27/27 tests; +1 regression test

   177	feedback-core checkRateLimit keys the in-memory map on

   191	## Concurrency proof (item 3 — true parallel RPC against real Postgres) 2026-05-19
   192	20 parallel same-stage claim_feedback_stage('id','triaged') on real PG ->
   193	distribution 19x 0 / 1x 1: exactly one transaction won (Read Committed
   194	re-evaluated WHERE against the row after the lock holder committed).
   195	Final state: triaged|triaged. Then 4 parallel DIFFERENT stages
   196	(shipped/triaged/in_progress/received) -> only shipped=1 (highest rank
   197	took the row lock first, others re-eval'd and returned 0). Final:
   198	shipped|shipped. Monotonic forward-only invariant holds under real race.

     1	# Surface — openfeedbacklayer (feat/feedback-journey)
     2	
     3	Generated: 2026-05-19  
     4	Repo SHA: $(git rev-parse --short HEAD)  
     5	Deployed URL: http://65.21.90.216:3025/
     6	
     7	## What this project is
     8	
     9	A drop-in React feedback widget + Next.js API routes + Supabase migrations.
    10	**This is a library**, not a webapp. The "deployed" URL is the
    11	`examples/nextjs` reference app used for testing only — production deployment
    12	is by host apps that install `openfeedbacklayer` from npm.
    13	
    14	## Pages (examples/nextjs reference app — the only public surface)
    15	
    16	- `GET /` — single demo page rendering the widget
    17	
    18	## API routes (the actual feature surface)
    19	
    20	- `POST /api/feedback` (multipart form):
    21	  - fields: `message` (req), `email` (opt), `subscribe` (opt 'true'),
    22	    `screenshot` (opt File ≤5MB), `project` (opt), `website` (honeypot — bots fill, real users don't)
    23	  - headers: `x-page-url`, `referer`, `x-forwarded-for`, `x-real-ip`, `user-agent`
    24	  - returns: `{id, ai_data, message}` 200 / `{error}` 400|429|500
    25	  - chain on success: rate-limit -> honeypot -> screenshot upload -> Gemini classify -> Postgres insert -> Octokit create issue (if GITHUB_TOKEN+GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO) -> Resend owner notification (if FEEDBACK_NOTIFY_EMAIL) -> Resend submitter confirmation (if subscribe+RESEND_API_KEY+issue created)
    26	
    27	- `POST /api/feedback/webhook` (application/json):
    28	  - body: GitHub Issues event JSON
    29	  - headers: `x-github-event: issues`, `x-hub-signature-256` (HMAC sha256)
    30	  - returns: `{ok:true}` 200 / `{error:'Invalid signature'}` 401
    31	  - chain: HMAC verify -> stage map (closed/labeled) -> row lookup by (github_repo, github_issue_number) -> forward-only RPC claim -> Resend stage email -> row update
    32	
    33	## UI components (the widget)
    34	
    35	- Floating bottom-right button (open/close)
    36	- Popup form (320×~75vh):
    37	  - Textarea (message, required)
    38	  - Email input (optional, type=email)
    39	  - Checkbox "Email me when this is addressed" (auto-checks when email typed; disabled when email empty)
    40	  - Screenshot button (file picker) + paste/drag support
    41	  - Send button (disabled until message non-empty)
    42	  - Honeypot input (hidden via off-screen position, tabindex=-1, aria-hidden)
    43	- Success state:
    44	  - ✓ checkmark
    45	  - AI summary text
    46	  - "We'll email you at X with updates." (when subscribed)
    47	  - Classification badges (category / feature_area / priority P0|P1|P2)
    48	  - Close button
    49	
    50	## MCP tools
    51	None.
    52	
    53	## CLI commands
    54	None.
    55	
    56	## Auth flows
    57	- None on the widget itself (anonymous submission by design).
    58	- API uses Supabase service_role JWT server-side only (never exposed to client).
    59	- Webhook auth: GitHub HMAC sha256 only.
    60	
    61	## Tech inventory
    62	
    63	- Library: TypeScript, React 18 (peer), tsup build, Node ≥18
    64	- Example app: Next.js 15.5.18 (App Router), React 18
    65	- Backend: `@supabase/supabase-js` (peer), PostgREST 12.2.3, Postgres 16
    66	- AI: `@google/generative-ai` 0.21 → Gemini 2.5 Flash Lite
    67	- GitHub: `@octokit/rest` 21 (peer)
    68	- Email: `resend` 4 (peer)
    69	- Crypto: `node:crypto` for HMAC
    70	
    71	## Personas (mapped to this surface)
    72	
    73	- **anonymous-visitor** (the only real persona — widget is for end users on a host app)
    74	- **mobile-visitor** — same widget on phone viewport
    75	- **automated-bot** — fills the honeypot or floods the rate limiter
    76	- **adversarial-submitter** — crafts message to leak PII to issue / inject markdown / phish via screenshot URL
    77	- **github-webhook-sender** (real GitHub OR an attacker forging signed payloads)
    78	- **integrator-developer** — npm-installs the package into their app (docs / DX persona)
    79	
    80	## Tested categories
    81	
    82	- Functional (widget submit chain end-to-end, real backend)
    83	- Auth + security (HMAC, RPC privilege, redaction, defang, ordering, ReDoS, rate-limit)
    84	- UI/UX (desktop + mobile screenshots, interaction)
    85	- Performance (load time, AI call time)
    86	- Data + DB (migration validity, journey state machine)
    87	- Email (Gmail-receipt verified, both confirmation + stage)
    88	- Documentation (README install + journey + security sections)
    89	- Disaster (GitHub down → fail graceful, Resend down → still 200, dead key → no throw)
    90	
    91	## N/A categories
    92	
    93	- SEO + sharing — library, no public site
    94	- Sandbox / runtime — no untrusted code execution
    95	- Trust + brand — no logged-in account, no transactions
    96	- Monitoring — no telemetry surface (consumers add their own)

     1	# Launch-Readiness Virgin-Session Report — claude-2026-05-19
     2	
     3	**URL:** http://65.21.90.216:3025/
     4	**Agent:** Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context)

     7	
     8	---
     9	
    10	## Summary
    11	
    12	The OpenFeedbackLayer feedback widget works end-to-end. The full submission pipeline (open → fill → send → AI classification → success state) succeeds on desktop, tablet, and mobile. The AI summarisation + categorisation is a genuine differentiator — it returns a polite human-readable summary plus category / feature-area / priority badges in ~2 seconds.
    13	
    14	The product is **functionally launch-ready**, but accessibility and visual polish have several real gaps that a designer or accessibility auditor will flag immediately. None of them block ship, but they hurt the "professional widget" perception.
    15	
    16	---
    17

    29	| 8 | Network: POST /api/feedback shape | PASS — `multipart/form-data` with `message`, `website` (honeypot), `subscribe`; response 200 with structured `ai_data` | get_network_request reqid 28, 29 |
    30	| 9 | Console | MOSTLY CLEAN — 1 dev-only HMR ws error, 1 production-relevant `__nextjs_font/geist-latin.woff2` 403, 1 a11y issue (form fields missing id/name) | list_console_messages |
    31	| 10 | Visual polish | OK but the missing Geist font is making the page render in Times New Roman fallback | inspection |
    32	
    33	---
    34	
    35	## Findings (Severity-Ordered)
    36	
    37	### P0 (blocks ship)
    38	
    39	**None.** The widget submits feedback successfully across viewports, returns sensible AI classifications, handles empty-message and no-email cases correctly, and does not crash on a 5000-char payload.
    40	
    41	### P1 (polish — fix before broad launch)
    42	
    43	**P1-1 — Geist font 403, page falls back to Times New Roman**
    44	The host page requests `/__nextjs_font/geist-latin.woff2` which returns **403 Forbidden** twice during the session. Result: `document.body` `font-family` resolves to `"Times New Roman"`. This is a Next.js font-pipeline misconfiguration. The widget itself uses `system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif` so it looks OK, but the example page heading is in a mono fallback and the body copy is in Times Roman, which makes the whole demo look broken.
    45	Repro: load the URL, open DevTools Network, filter font, see `geist-latin.woff2 — 403`.
    46	Screenshot: `/tmp/lr-claude-01-desktop-home.png`
    47	
    48	**P1-2 — Escape key does NOT close the popup**
    49	Pressing Escape (dispatched on `document`, `window`, and the focused element) leaves the widget open. This is a standard expectation for any dismissible popover.
    50	Repro: open widget, press Escape — widget remains visible.
    51	
    52	**P1-3 — Focus is not returned to the FAB after close**
    53	After clicking the × button or Close button, `document.activeElement` is `<body>`, not the trigger FAB. Screen-reader and keyboard users lose their place.
    54	Repro: tab to FAB → Enter → fill → Send → Close. Focus is on body.
    55	
    56	**P1-4 — No `role="dialog"`, no `aria-modal`, no focus trap**
    57	The popup is just a `<div>` — screen readers will not announce "dialog opened", users can tab out of the popup into the page behind it (which has no other interactive elements here, so it's invisible to the eye, but still wrong).
    58	Repro: `evaluate_script` — `document.querySelector('[role="dialog"]')` returns null, ditto `[aria-modal]`.
    59	
    60	**P1-5 — Close button has no accessible name**
    61	The `×` button (uid 14_1) renders the literal character `×` with no `aria-label`. Screen readers read this as "multiplication sign" or similar. Should be `aria-label="Close feedback"`.
    62	Repro: inspection — `closeBtn.getAttribute('aria-label')` returns `null`.
    63	
    64	**P1-6 — Close button touch target is 19x29px (well below 44px)**
    65	The × close button is 19px wide × 29px tall. Apple HIG / WCAG 2.5.5 ask for 44x44 minimum. Easy mistap on mobile.
    66	Repro: inspection — `closeBtn.offsetWidth=19, offsetHeight=29`.
    67	
    68	**P1-7 — FAB lacks `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-controls`**
    69	The FAB button toggles a popover but does not advertise that to assistive tech.
    70	Repro: inspection — all three attributes are `null` on the FAB.
    71	
    72	**P1-8 — Form fields missing `id` and `name` attributes**
    73	Chrome's a11y panel raises "A form field element should have an id or name attribute" (count 9 after the full session). Autofill and screen readers depend on these.
    74	Repro: `list_console_messages` includes the issue.
    75	
    76	**P1-9 — Close (×) button has `type="submit"` and lives outside a `<form>`**
    77	The × button has `type="submit"` but there is no `<form>` wrapper. The Send button also has `type="submit"` but no form. No native form submission semantics, so Enter-in-textbox doesn't submit and there's no degraded JS-off behaviour. It also means the close × theoretically could trigger an unintended submit if a form ever got added around it. Cosmetic but fragile.
    78	
    79	### P2 (nice to have)
    80	
    81	**P2-1 — HMR WebSocket noise in console**
    82	`ws://65.21.90.216:3025/_next/webpack-hmr` fails on every load. This is the dev server's hot-reload socket; in a production build it would not exist. If this URL is the public preview, run `next build && next start` instead of `next dev` to remove these.
    83	
    84	**P2-2 — Send button height 36px is below the 44px touch guideline**
    85	Send is 140x36. Width is fine, height is a hair short. Mobile mistap risk is low because of the comfortable horizontal target.
    86	
    87	**P2-3 — Color of FAB button text is `rgb(0, 0, 0)` by default**
    88	The SVG icon overrides with `stroke="white"`, so this is harmless. But if a future contributor adds text to the FAB, they will get black-on-blue (contrast ratio ~3.7, fails AA). Set `color: #fff` on the button explicitly.
    89	
    90	**P2-4 — Email line wrapping on mobile uses a hard hyphen-break**
    91	On the mobile success state, the email `depontefede+lr-claude-mobile@gmail.com` wraps mid-token (after `+lr-`). Looks OK but a `word-break: break-all` or `overflow-wrap: anywhere` would feel more intentional than a hyphen-split.
    92	Screenshot: `/tmp/lr-claude-08-mobile-success.png`
    93	
    94	**P2-5 — No visible character counter for the textarea**
    95	Pasting 5000 chars works fine, but the user has no idea if there's a limit. A simple `Math.max(0, 5000 - value.length)` counter under the textarea would help.
    96	
    97	---
    98	
    99	## Three Things That Work Especially Well
   100	
   101	1. **AI summarisation + classification is the killer feature.** The success state shows a polite, human-readable rewrite of the user's complaint plus three badges (category / feature-area / priority). My "Demo test from launch-readiness — please ignore" came back as "User has submitted a test entry for launch-readiness and requests it be ignored" with badges `other / launch-readiness / P2`. The 5000-char Lorem Ipsum came back with "The user has provided a large amount of placeholder text, which does not contain actionable feedback" plus two clarifying questions — sensible, not naive. This is the moment a virgin user goes "oh, this is different".
   102	2. **Empty-state handling is correct everywhere.** Send is disabled until message is non-empty (no nag dialog, no alert). Email is genuinely optional — the success state shows the "We'll email you..." line only when email is provided. The subscribe checkbox starts disabled and auto-checks when email is entered. This is small but a lot of widgets get it wrong.
   103	3. **No layout pollution at any viewport.** The popup is `position: fixed; bottom: 20px; right: 20px; z-index: 99999` with a soft `0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.2)` shadow and 12px border radius. At 375px, 768px, 1280px it stays anchored, never overflows the viewport, the textarea scrolls internally on big content rather than growing the card. POST is multipart/form-data with a `website` honeypot field — solid anti-spam baseline. No console hydration errors, no React strict-mode warnings, no source-map fails.
   104	
   105	---
   106

   108	
   109	```
   110	POST http://65.21.90.216:3025/api/feedback
   111	Content-Type: multipart/form-data
   112	Body fields: message, website (honeypot), subscribe ("true"|"false"), email (when provided)
   113	Response 200 application/json:

   125	    "confidence": 0.9,
   126	    "clarifying_questions": [...]
   127	  },
   128	  "message": "Feedback received"
   129	}
   130	```
   131	
   132	Latency observed: ~1.5–2.5s per request (AI inference dominates).

   129	## Plan Mode Safe Operations
   130	
   131	In plan mode, allowed because they inform the plan: `$B`, `$D`, `codex exec`/`codex review`, writes to `~/.gstack/`, writes to the plan file, and `open` for generated artifacts.
   132	
   133	## Skill Invocation During Plan Mode
   134	
   135	If the user invokes a skill in plan mode, the skill takes precedence over generic plan mode behavior. **Treat the skill file as executable instructions, not reference.** Follow it step by step starting from Step 0; the first AskUserQuestion is the workflow entering plan mode, not a violation of it. AskUserQuestion (any variant — `mcp__*__AskUserQuestion` or native; see "AskUserQuestion Format → Tool resolution") satisfies plan mode's end-of-turn requirement. If no variant is callable, fall back to writing the decision brief into the plan file as a `## Decisions to confirm` section + ExitPlanMode — never silently auto-decide. At a STOP point, stop immediately. Do not continue the workflow or call ExitPlanMode there. Commands marked "PLAN MODE EXCEPTION — ALWAYS RUN" execute. Call ExitPlanMode only after the skill workflow completes, or if the user tells you to cancel the skill or leave plan mode.
   136	
   137	If `PROACTIVE` is `"false"`, do not auto-invoke or proactively suggest skills. If a skill seems useful, ask: "I think /skillname might help here — want me to run it?"
   138	
   139	If `SKILL_PREFIX` is `"true"`, suggest/invoke `/gstack-*` names. Disk paths stay `~/.claude/skills/gstack/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`.
   140	
   141	If output shows `UPGRADE_AVAILABLE <old> <new>`: read `~/.claude/skills/gstack/gstack-upgrade/SKILL.md` and follow the "Inline upgrade flow" (auto-upgrade if configured, otherwise AskUserQuestion with 4 options, write snooze state if declined).
   142	
   143	If output shows `JUST_UPGRADED <from> <to>`: print "Running gstack v{to} (just updated!)". If `SPAWNED_SESSION` is true, skip feature discovery.
   144	
   145	Feature discovery, max one prompt per session:
   146	- Missing `~/.claude/skills/gstack/.feature-prompted-continuous-checkpoint`: AskUserQuestion for Continuous checkpoint auto-commits. If accepted, run `~/.claude/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-config set checkpoint_mode continuous`. Always touch marker.
   147	- Missing `~/.claude/skills/gstack/.feature-prompted-model-overlay`: inform "Model overlays are active. MODEL_OVERLAY shows the patch." Always touch marker.
   148	
   149	After upgrade prompts, continue workflow.
   150

   152	
   153	> v1 prompts are simpler: first-use jargon glosses, outcome-framed questions, shorter prose. Keep default or restore terse?
   154	
   155	Options:
   156	- A) Keep the new default (recommended — good writing helps everyone)
   157	- B) Restore V0 prose — set `explain_level: terse`
   158

   160	If B: run `~/.claude/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-config set explain_level terse`.

   167

   171

   173	open https://garryslist.org/posts/boil-the-ocean

   175	```
   176	
   177	Only run `open` if yes. Always run `touch`.

   182

   185	- B) No thanks

   198	If B→B: run `~/.claude/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-config set telemetry off`
   199

     1	Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase. Let me compile my virgin-session review.
     2	
     3	---
     4	
     5	# OpenFeedbackLayer — Virgin-Session Review
     6	
     7	**Overall Score: 62 / 100**
     8	
     9	| Category | Score | Max | Notes |
    10	|----------|-------|-----|-------|
    11	| **Install DX** | 14 | 25 | Manual copy-paste steps, missing peer dep guidance, no CLI/bootstrap |
    12	| **Copy Quality** | 9 | 15 | Inconsistent tone, placeholder fallback copy, weak error UX |
    13	| **API Contract Clarity** | 16 | 25 | Types are decent but `FeedbackDeps.supabase: any`, missing runtime validation, implicit env contract |
    14	| **Security Hygiene (from reading)** | 15 | 20 | Excellent redaction pipeline, honest rate-limit caveat, but `supabase: any` weakens injection safety |
    15	| **Gaps** | 8 | 15 | No migration runner, no health check, no retry/backoff, missing docs on non-Next.js usage |
    16	
    17	---
    18	
    19	## 3–5 P1 Polish Gaps (with exact evidence)
    20	
    21	### 1. Install DX: Manual file copy is a friction cliff
    22	> **Evidence:** README step 3 says `cp node_modules/openfeedbacklayer/src/api/feedback/route.ts app/api/feedback/route.ts`.  
    23	> The `files` array in `package.json` includes `"src"`, so this *works*, but requiring users to `cp` from `node_modules` is brittle (path changes break on upgrade, users must know their framework's route convention). A CLI scaffold (`npx openfeedbacklayer init`) or at least documented `curl` templates would eliminate this. The `src/api/feedback/route.ts` file itself imports `NextRequest` — it's Next.js-coupled with no mention of how to adapt for Remix, SvelteKit, or raw Node.
    24	
    25	### 2. `FeedbackDeps.supabase: any` undermines the entire type contract
    26	> **Evidence:** `src/server/feedback-core.ts` line 10: `supabase: any`.  
    27	> The server core's primary dependency is typed as `any`. This means zero IntelliSense for `.from('feedback').insert(...)`, no compile-time safety on table/column names, and no protection against API drift. For a package that asks users to wire server-side handlers, this is a significant API-contract hole. The `tsconfig.json` has `strict: true`, yet the most critical server interface opts out.
    28	
    29	### 3. Widget shows "Thank you for your feedback!" even on total failure
    30	> **Evidence:** `src/components/FeedbackWidget.tsx` lines 194–207.  
    31	> In the `catch` block, the widget sets `isSent = true` and displays `aiResponse = 'Thank you for your feedback!'` regardless of whether the POST actually succeeded. The `onError` callback fires, but the UI lies to the user. A user who just lost their carefully-typed bug report sees a green checkmark. The `console.error` is invisible to non-dev users. This is a trust-destroying UX bug.
    32	
    33	### 4. Rate limiter is in-memory and non-distributed with a documented but unmitigated spoofing risk
    34	> **Evidence:** `src/server/feedback-core.ts` lines 40–48 and 46–69.  
    35	> The code includes an admirably honest security comment: *"x-forwarded-for / x-real-ip are client-supplied... an attacker can rotate the header to bypass the cap"*. However, the mitigation is "deploy behind a trusted proxy" — there is no runtime enforcement or warning. On Vercel (the implied target), this is fine; on a self-hosted Node server, it's trivially bypassable. More critically, the `rateLimitMap` is a process-local `Map` — it resets on every cold start and does not coordinate across serverless instances. A determined spammer can hit 10 requests per instance per minute, multiplied by however many Vercel regions spin up.
    36	
    37	### 5. Public Supabase Storage bucket with no size validation server-side
    38	> **Evidence:** `supabase/migrations/003_screenshot_storage.sql` lines 20–26:  
    39	> `CREATE POLICY "Allow public uploads to feedback bucket" ON storage.objects FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (bucket_id = 'feedback');`  
    40	> The bucket has a 5MB `file_size_limit`, but the widget's client-side check (`file.size > 5 * 1024 * 1024`) can be bypassed by forging the request. The server (`feedback-core.ts` lines 150–170) does upload the file but never validates `screenshot.size` against a server-side limit before calling Supabase. The `contentType` is hardcoded to `'image/png'` regardless of actual file type — a bypassed JPEG/WebP gets mislabeled.
    41	
    42	---
    43	
    44	## 2–3 Things That Genuinely Work Well
    45	
    46	### 1. The GitHub issue redaction pipeline is impressively thorough
    47	> **Evidence:** `src/lib/github-service.ts` lines 27–207.  
    48	> The redaction system shows real security craftsmanship: NFKC normalization, bounded quantifiers (ReDoS defense), Unicode-aware email/phone regexes with `/u` and `\p{L}\p{M}\p{N}`, control-character stripping, markdown defanging with zero-width spaces, URL sanitization with high-entropy path detection, and explicit comments explaining *why* each step exists. The `UNSAFE_URL` fallback and the honest disclaimer *"best-effort defense-in-depth, not a guarantee"* show mature security communication.
    49	
    50	### 2. Atomic stage-claim RPC with proper privilege revocation
    51	> **Evidence:** `supabase/migrations/004_claim_stage_rpc.sql` lines 30–66.  
    52	> The webhook journey uses a PostgreSQL function with `IS DISTINCT FROM` for NULL-safe comparison, forward-only rank checking, and `RETURNING` for atomic claim semantics. The migration explicitly revokes `EXECUTE` from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, and `authenticated`, then grants only to `service_role`. This is exactly how you should build a concurrent-safe, least-privilege webhook handler. The inline comment even explains the prior bug (PostgREST rejecting `or` on mutation).
    53	
    54	### 3. Graceful AI degradation with structured fallback
    55	> **Evidence:** `src/lib/ai-service.ts` lines 19–27 and 121–124; `src/components/FeedbackWidget.tsx` lines 166–180.  
    56	> If Gemini fails, returns garbage, or the key is missing, the system falls back to a default classification (`category: 'feedback'`, `priority: 'P1'`) instead of crashing. The server returns `ai_data: null` cleanly, and the widget handles it without throwing. This "AI is optional" posture is the right default for a feedback widget — users should never lose their submission because an LLM hiccuped.
    57	
    58	---
    59	
    60	## Honest Summary
    61	
    62	OpenFeedbackLayer is a **competent, security-conscious package** with a genuinely well-built redaction and journey pipeline. The GitHub webhook flow and atomic stage claiming show real engineering care. However, it ships with **friction-heavy install DX** (manual file copies), a **type-system hole at its server core** (`supabase: any`), and a **widget that lies to users on failure**. These are fixable, but they are real barriers to adoption and trust. The security hygiene is above average for an open-source widget, but the operational gaps (in-memory rate limiting, no server-side upload size enforcement) mean it's not production-hardened out of the box.

Code:
     5	# OpenFeedbackLayer
     6	
     7	Open-source feedback widget with AI classification. A drop-in React component that captures user feedback, automatically classifies it using Gemini AI, and stores it in Supabase.
     8	
     9	## Features
    10	
    11	- **Floating feedback button** - Non-intrusive, appears in corner of screen
    12	- **Screenshot support** - Paste, drag & drop, or click to attach images
    13	- **AI classification** - Automatically categorizes feedback (bug/feature/question/billing/praise)
    14	- **Feature area detection** - AI identifies which part of your product is mentioned
    15	- **Priority scoring** - P0/P1/P2 based on urgency
    16	- **Bot protection** - Honeypot field + rate limiting
    17	- **Email notifications** - Get notified via Resend on new feedback
    18	- **Supabase storage** - Screenshots stored in Supabase Storage
    19	
    20	## Quick Start
    21	
    22	### 1. Install the package
    23	
    24	```bash
    25	npm install openfeedbacklayer @supabase/supabase-js
    26	# or
    27	pnpm add openfeedbacklayer @supabase/supabase-js
    28	# or
    29	yarn add openfeedbacklayer @supabase/supabase-js
    30	```
    31	
    32	### 2. Add the widget to your app
    33	
    34	```tsx
    35	// app/layout.tsx or any layout component
    36	import { FeedbackWidget } from openfeedbacklayer
    37	
    38	export default function Layout({ children }) {
    39	  return (
    40	    <html>
    41	      <body>
    42	        {children}
    43	        <FeedbackWidget />
    44	      </body>
    45	    </html>
    46	  )
    47	}
    48	```
    49	
    50	### 3. Set up the API route
    51	
    52	Copy the API route template to your Next.js app:
    53	
    54	```bash
    55	cp node_modules/openfeedbacklayer/src/api/feedback/route.ts app/api/feedback/route.ts
    56	```
    57	
    58	Or create `app/api/feedback/route.ts` manually - see [API Route Template](./src/api/feedback/route.ts).
    59	
    60	### 4. Set up Supabase
    61	
    62	Run the migrations in order in your Supabase SQL Editor:
    63	
    64	```bash
    65	ls node_modules/openfeedbacklayer/supabase/migrations
    66	```
    67	
    68	- `001_create_feedback.sql` and `002_feedback_journey.sql` are required. They
    69	  apply on any Postgres (no Supabase-specific objects).
    70	- `004_claim_stage_rpc.sql` is required if you use the subscriber journey
    71	  (GitHub webhook stage emails). Without it the webhook cannot claim a stage
    72	  and subscriber update emails are silently not sent.
    73	- `003_screenshot_storage.sql` is optional: run it only if you use screenshot
    74	  attachments. It requires Supabase Storage.
    75	
    76	Apply them in numeric order (`001`, `002`, `004`; `003` whenever you enable
    77	screenshots).
    78	
    79	Or see [Migration SQL](./supabase/migrations).
    80	
    81	### 5. Add environment variables
    82	
    83	```env
    84	# .env.local
    85	
    86	# Required
    87	SUPABASE_URL=https://xxx.supabase.co
    88	SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=eyJxxx
    89	
    90	# For AI classification
    91	GEMINI_API_KEY=AIzaSyxxx
    92	
    93	# Optional: Email notifications
    94	RESEND_API_KEY=re_xxx
    95	FEEDBACK_NOTIFY_EMAIL=you@example.com
    96	RESEND_FROM_EMAIL=feedback@yourdomain.com
    97	
    98	# Optional: GitHub feedback journey
    99	GITHUB_TOKEN=github_pat_xxx
   100	GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO=owner/name
   101	GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET=change-me
   102	```
   103	
   104	For GitHub issue creation and subscriber email updates, install `@octokit/rest` and `resend` in the host app when those features are enabled.
   105	
   106	## Configuration
   107	
   108	### Widget Props
   109	
   110	```tsx
   111	<FeedbackWidget
   112	  apiEndpoint="/api/feedback"       // API endpoint (default: /api/feedback)
   113	  projectId="my-app"                // For multi-project setups
   114	  position="bottom-right"           // bottom-right | bottom-left | top-right | top-left
   115	  primaryColor="#2563eb"            // Button color
   116	  buttonText="Feedback"             // Tooltip text
   117	  placeholder="Describe your issue..." // Input placeholder
   118	  collectEmail={true}               // Collect optional subscriber email
   119	  emailPlaceholder="Your email..."  // Email input placeholder
   120	  onSubmit={(data) => {}}           // Callback after submit
   121	  onError={(error) => {}}           // Error callback
   122	/>
   123	```
   124	
   125	## Feedback journey (GitHub + subscriber updates)
   126	
   127	Set `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_FEEDBACK_REPO` to auto-open a GitHub issue for each feedback item. Install `@octokit/rest` and `resend` in the host app when GitHub issue creation and subscriber emails are enabled.
   128	
   129	To email subscribers as feedback moves through the journey, configure a GitHub webhook:
   130	
   131	- URL: `/api/feedback/webhook`
   132	- Content type: `application/json`
   133	- Events: Issues
   134	- Secret: `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`
   135	
   136	Subscribers receive concise updates for received, triaged, in progress, shipped, and wont fix stages. Submitter email is stored only in Supabase and is never added to the public GitHub issue. GitHub issue redaction is best-effort defense-in-depth, not a guarantee, because the issue body can still include user-typed content.

--- supabase/migrations/003_screenshot_storage.sql
     1	-- OpenFeedbackLayer: OPTIONAL screenshot storage
     2	--
     3	-- Run this ONLY if you want screenshot attachments. It requires Supabase
     4	-- Storage (the `storage` schema). The core feedback table (001) and the
     5	-- journey columns (002) do NOT depend on this; skip this file if you do not
     6	-- use screenshots or are not on Supabase.
     7	
     8	-- Create storage bucket for screenshots
     9	INSERT INTO storage.buckets (id, name, public, file_size_limit, allowed_mime_types)
    10	VALUES (
    11	  'feedback',
    12	  'feedback',
    13	  true,
    14	  5242880,  -- 5MB max
    15	  ARRAY['image/png', 'image/jpeg', 'image/gif', 'image/webp']
    16	)
    17	ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING;
    18	
    19	-- Storage policy: allow public uploads
    20	CREATE POLICY "Allow public uploads to feedback bucket"
    21	  ON storage.objects FOR INSERT
    22	  WITH CHECK (bucket_id = 'feedback');
    23	
    24	-- Storage policy: allow public reads
    25	CREATE POLICY "Allow public reads from feedback bucket"
    26	  ON storage.objects FOR SELECT
    27	  USING (bucket_id = 'feedback');
--- supabase/migrations/004_claim_stage_rpc.sql
     1	-- OpenFeedbackLayer: atomic, forward-only stage-claim for the webhook journey.
     2	--
     3	-- The webhook must advance journey_stage forward-only and emit exactly one
     4	-- subscriber email per stage even under concurrent / duplicate / out-of-order
     5	-- GitHub deliveries. A check-then-write in application code races; a PostgREST
     6	-- `or` filter on a mutation is rejected (SQLSTATE 42703). This function does
     7	-- the whole decision in one atomic statement:
     8	--   * last_emailed_stage IS DISTINCT FROM p_stage  -> not already emailed
     9	--     (NULL-safe: a never-emailed row is claimable)
    10	--   * rank(p_stage) > rank(current journey_stage)   -> strictly forward only,
    11	--     so a late 'triaged' cannot regress a row already at 'shipped'
    12	-- It returns the row id only to the caller that won the claim; concurrent or
    13	-- duplicate or backward deliveries get zero rows and send no email.
    14	
    15	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION ofl_stage_rank(p_stage text)
    16	RETURNS int
    17	LANGUAGE sql
    18	IMMUTABLE
    19	AS $$
    20	  SELECT CASE p_stage
    21	    WHEN 'received'    THEN 0
    22	    WHEN 'triaged'     THEN 1
    23	    WHEN 'in_progress' THEN 2
    24	    WHEN 'shipped'     THEN 3
    25	    WHEN 'wont_fix'    THEN 3
    26	    ELSE -1
    27	  END;
    28	$$;
    29	
    30	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION claim_feedback_stage(p_id uuid, p_stage text)
    31	RETURNS TABLE (id uuid)
    32	LANGUAGE sql
    33	AS $$
    34	  UPDATE feedback
    35	     SET journey_stage = p_stage,
    36	         last_emailed_stage = p_stage
    37	   WHERE feedback.id = p_id
    38	     AND feedback.last_emailed_stage IS DISTINCT FROM p_stage
    39	     AND ofl_stage_rank(p_stage)
    40	         > ofl_stage_rank(COALESCE(feedback.journey_stage, 'received'))
    41	  RETURNING feedback.id;
    42	$$;
    43	
    44	COMMENT ON FUNCTION claim_feedback_stage(uuid, text) IS
    45	  'OpenFeedbackLayer: atomic forward-only claim of a feedback journey stage; returns id only to the winning caller.';
    46	
    47	-- Server-only. Supabase grants EXECUTE on new functions to PUBLIC (which
    48	-- includes anon and authenticated) by default; revoke that and grant only the
    49	-- service_role used by the server-side webhook handler, so the public REST
    50	-- API cannot drive journey state.
    51	REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION ofl_stage_rank(text) FROM PUBLIC;
    52	REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION claim_feedback_stage(uuid, text) FROM PUBLIC;
    53	DO $$
    54	BEGIN
    55	  IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'anon') THEN
    56	    EXECUTE 'REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION ofl_stage_rank(text) FROM anon';
    57	    EXECUTE 'REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION claim_feedback_stage(uuid, text) FROM anon';
    58	  END IF;
    59	  IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'authenticated') THEN
    60	    EXECUTE 'REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION ofl_stage_rank(text) FROM authenticated';
    61	    EXECUTE 'REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION claim_feedback_stage(uuid, text) FROM authenticated';
    62	  END IF;
    63	  IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'service_role') THEN
    64	    EXECUTE 'GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION claim_feedback_stage(uuid, text) TO service_role';
    65	  END IF;
    66	END $$;

     1	import test from 'node:test'

    10	function isNpmUnavailable(error) {
    11	  const output = `${error.stdout || ''}\n${error.stderr || ''}\n${error.message || ''}`
    12	  return /ENOTFOUND|EAI_AGAIN|ECONNRESET|ETIMEDOUT|network|fetch failed|could not resolve/i.test(output)
    13	}
    14	
    15	test('published package contract', async (t) => {
    16	  let tarballPath = null
    17	  let tempDir = null
    18	
    19	  try {
    20	    execFileSync('npm', ['--version'], { stdio: 'ignore' })
    21	  } catch (error) {
    22	    t.skip(`npm unavailable: ${error.message}`)
    23	    return
    24	  }
    25	
    26	  try {
    27	    const tarballName = execFileSync('npm', ['pack', '--silent'], {
    28	      cwd: repoRoot,
    29	      encoding: 'utf8',
    30	      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
    31	    }).trim().split('\n').at(-1)
    32	    tarballPath = join(repoRoot, tarballName)
    33	    tempDir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'openfeedbacklayer-consumer-'))
    34	
    35	    execFileSync('npm', ['init', '-y'], {
    36	      cwd: tempDir,
    37	      stdio: 'ignore',
    38	    })
    39	
    40	    try {
    41	      execFileSync('npm', ['install', '--silent', tarballPath, '@supabase/supabase-js', '@octokit/rest', 'resend'], {
    42	        cwd: tempDir,
    43	        stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
    44	      })
    45	    } catch (error) {
    46	      if (isNpmUnavailable(error)) {
    47	        t.skip(`npm registry unavailable: ${error.message}`)
    48	        return
    49	      }
    50	      throw error
    51	    }
    52	
    53	    const output = execFileSync(
    54	      'node',
    55	      ['-e', "import('openfeedbacklayer').then(()=>import('openfeedbacklayer/server')).then(()=>console.log('OK'))"],
    56	      {
    57	        cwd: tempDir,

    60	      },

    62

I’m doing a headless browser pass now to confirm the widget is actually visible and interactive, not only server-rendered text in HTML.
exec
/bin/bash -lc "node --input-type=module <<'EOF'
import { chromium } from 'playwright'
import { mkdirSync } from 'node:fs'
mkdirSync('/tmp/ofl-review', { recursive: true })
const browser = await chromium.launch({ headless: true })
const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1280, height: 800 } })
const errors = []
page.on('console', msg => { if (msg.type() === 'error') errors.push(msg.text()) })
page.on('pageerror', err => errors.push(err.message))
await page.goto('http://65.21.90.216:3025/', { waitUntil: 'networkidle', timeout: 20000 })
await page.screenshot({ path: '/tmp/ofl-review/live-home.png', fullPage: true })
await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Feedback' }).click()
await page.getByPlaceholder('Describe your feedback, bug, or feature request...').fill('Pre-landing browser interaction check')
await page.getByPlaceholder('Your email (optional, to hear back)').fill('reviewer@example.com')
const checkbox = page.getByLabel('Email me when this is addressed')
await checkbox.waitFor({ state: 'visible' })
await page.screenshot({ path: '/tmp/ofl-review/live-widget-open.png', fullPage: true })
const state = {
  title: await page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'OpenFeedbackLayer Next.js Example' }).isVisible(),
  feedbackButtonVisible: await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Feedback' }).isVisible().catch(() => false),
  sendButtonEnabled: await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Send' }).isEnabled(),
  checkboxChecked: await checkbox.isChecked(),
  consoleErrors: errors,
}
console.log(JSON.stringify(state, null, 2))
await browser.close()
EOF" in /root/openfeedbacklayer

codex
Findings first: no P0/P1 ship-holds found.

[P2] Live URL is running a dev Next server.
Evidence: Playwright captured a console error for `/_next/webpack-hmr`, and the HTML includes Next dev tooling. Fix: run the demo URL with `next build && next start` or equivalent production process before treating that URL as production evidence.

[P2] Confirmation/notification emails are not awaited in the feedback POST path.
Evidence: [src/server/feedback-core.ts](/root/openfeedbacklayer/src/server/feedback-core.ts:238) and [src/server/feedback-core.ts](/root/openfeedbacklayer/src/server/feedback-core.ts:249) start async email work without waiting for completion. Fix: `await` those calls or use the platform’s background-work primitive.

[P2] Example app dependency metadata is stale/incomplete for Supabase.
Evidence: [examples/nextjs/package.json](/root/openfeedbacklayer/examples/nextjs/package.json:68) omits `@supabase/supabase-js`, while the example routes import `openfeedbacklayer/server`. Fix: add `@supabase/supabase-js` to the example dependencies and regenerate the example lockfile.

[P2] Untracked launch-readiness docs contain failed command output.
Evidence: [nvidia-deepseek-2026-05-19.md](/root/openfeedbacklayer/docs/launch-readiness/agent-runs/nvidia-deepseek-2026-05-19.md:65) includes shell errors. Fix: omit these generated docs from the commit or regenerate clean artifacts.

Verification performed:
`npm run type-check` passed. `npm test` passed: 33/33. `npm audit --omit=dev` found 0 vulnerabilities. `npm pack` includes `dist`, route templates, and migrations `001`-`004`. Clean ESM and CJS consumer imports passed. Live URL checks: GET `/` 200, empty feedback POST 400, honeypot POST 200 fake success, unsigned webhook 401. Browser screenshot evidence: `/tmp/ofl-review/live-home.png`, `/tmp/ofl-review/live-widget-open.png`.

Tooling caveat: `npm run lint` fails because `eslint` is not installed. The local gstack review checklist file was also unavailable, so I applied the requested review categories directly.

Self-audit performed: API contract, package contract, security, deploy/migration path, README accuracy, types, dead code, naming, verification outputs, and live URL behavior were reviewed.

VERDICT SHIP. Score: 91/100.
